2a:["$","p",null,{"children":["Quantum teleportation is a technique in quantum physics that allows the transfer of quantum information from one location to another without physically moving particles. Unlike the sci-fi concept of teleportation, this process doesn't involve transporting matter. Instead, it relies on the principle of quantum entanglement, where two particles become linked regardless of distance. Through a series of precise measurements and classical communication, the quantum state of one particle can be recreated in another particle at a distant location, effectively \"teleporting\" the quantum information. In this module, we'll see how this works mathematically, and then we will implement quantum teleportation on a real quantum computer. The introduction here will be brief; for more background on quantum information, and more explanation about teleportation, we recommend John Watrous's course on the ",["$","$L1d",null,{"href":"/learning/courses/basics-of-quantum-information","children":"Basics of quantum information"}],", and in particular the section on ",["$","$L1d",null,{"href":"/learning/courses/basics-of-quantum-information/entanglement-in-action/quantum-teleportation","children":"Teleportation"}],"."],"className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}]

38:["$","p",null,{"children":"The entangled of qubits in this way does not require that the qubits remain physically close to one another.
